### J6b4d4ddf8645b289

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Clause 15 — Reinforcement|Clause 15 — Reinforcement]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "15",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 40,
>       "text": "15.   REINFORCEMENT:  Steel reinforcement shall be either mild steel of tested quality high\n        yield strength deformed bars of grade Fe-415 conforming to IS; 1786--1966, Fe-500 or\n      as called for on the drawings Fabric reinforcement where called for shall be of hard drawn\n       mild steel wire mesh conforming to IS: 1566-1967. Bars shall be free from deleterious\n        materials, mill scale, loose rust, oil or paint. The contractor shall submit bar bending\n       schedules for approval of the Engineer prior to commencement of fabrication. These shall\n        indicate the accurate dimensions and bending of bars as called for on the structural\n       drawings. Fabrication shall be accurately done to the dimensions, spacing and ensuring\n      minimum cover as called for on structural drawings. All steel shall be rigidly held in place\n       with 18 gauge annealed steel wire. Cement mortar (1;2) blocks of required shape, M.S.\n       chairs and spacer bars shall be used  in order to ensure accurate positioning  of\n       reinforcement.\n         All reinforcing steel within the limit of a day's polish shall be in place and firmly wired at\n        least one day, prior to the date of pour to permit inspection. The contractor shall also\n       ensure that all conduits and inserts are in position before placing concrete.\n      Hooks where indicated shall be either a complete semi-circular turn with a radius of not\n        less than four and not more than six bar's diameters, plus an extension of at least four\n       bar diameters at the free end, or a 90-degree bend having a radius of not less than 4 bar\n       diameters plus an extension of 12 bar diameters. Unless otherwise shown or specified\n      on the drawings, all lapped by 50 times the diameter of bar. Not more than 33%. (Thirty-\n      Three Percent) of the bars shall have lapped joints at the same location. Only where\n        specifically shown on the drawings, reinforcement shall have welded joints. Rate quoted\n         for steel reinforcement work shall be deemed to include the cost of such welding\n       wherever specified."
>     }
>   ],

### J7e03572adbaca19a

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Clause 13 — Strength of Ordinary Concrete|Clause 13 — Strength of Ordinary Concrete]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "13",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 37,
>       "text": "13.   STRENGTH OF ORDINARY CONCRETE:       The contractors have to see that\n             proper materials are used and the proportion and the correct water cement ratio just\n                sufficient for the workability are maintained to see that the following minimum strength of\n             concrete are obtained.\n           To verify this, test cubes from the concrete used should be made and tested. The\n             frequency of testing and the acceptability criteria will be according to IS 456 and IS 516."
>     }
>   ],
>   "heading_line": "      13.   STRENGTH OF ORDINARY CONCRETE:       The contractors have to see that",

### J920a9f596916d4bf

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Clause 14 — Concrete Formwork and Centering|Clause 14 — Concrete Formwork and Centering]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "14",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 37,
>       "text": "14   CONCRETE FORMWORK AND CENTERING:\n\n      14.1   General:\n           Form work shall be rigidly constructed of 12 mm thick new plastic coated plywood sheets\n                for column, pedestals, beams, walls and fascia and steel or plywood for roof slab,\n             canopies, floors, cover slabs, retaining walls, etc. Shuttering faces in contact with the\n             concrete shall be free from adhering grout, projecting nails, splits or other defects that\n             are likely to mar the concrete surface. The shuttering shall be erected on battens, beams\n           and steel props properly cross-braced so as to make the form work rigid.\n           Form work shall be erected true to line and levels and to the required shapes as called\n                for and shall be able to carry without deformation the full weight of wet concrete and live\n              loads. It shall also withstand the effect of vibration without deflection, bulging, distortion\n              or loosening of its component parts. Details of centering and form work shall be subject\n               to the approval of the Engineer. The completed form work shall also be subject to the\n             approval by the Engineer before placement of reinforcement. Contractor shall be\n              responsible for sufficiency and adequacy of all form work centering and moulds.\n                 All joints shall be sufficiently tight to prevent leakage of grout. Chamfer fillets shall be\n             provided at all corners wherever called for on the drawings or as directed by the Engineer.\n            Openings may .be given with the approval of Engineer at convenient places for washing\n           down all the rubbish. These are to be closed before concreting.\n\n      14.2  Surface Treatment of Shuttering:  The surfaces of shuttering coming into contact with\n             concrete shall be plastic coated of approved make. The shuttering shall be thoroughly\n            cleaned and coated before each use.\n      14.3  Removal of Form Work:     All formwork shall be removed without shock or vibration\n           and shall be eased off carefully in order to allow the structure to take up its load gradually.\n           Forms shall not be disturbed until, concrete had adequately hardened to take up its own\n             weight and superimposed load coming on it and in no circumstance shall forms be struck\n                 until the concrete reaches strength of at least twice the stress to which the concrete may\n             subjected at the time of striking. The side forms shall be so fixed that while removing\n           them the supporting forms & posts are not disturbed.\n      14.4  Surface Finish of Concrete:\n            Only cement mortar rendering of maximum 6 mm thickness may be permitted as finishing\n               to concrete surface. Imperfect surfaces shall not be patched but shall be repaired by\n            removal and replacement of the member as directed by the Engineer."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 38,
>       "text": "If centering and shuttering is to be paid for separately, measurement will be taken\n       according to IS 1200. The unit area for payment will be one square meter.\n         All aggregate are to conform strictly to IS specification IS 383. The aggregates will be\n       tested as frequently as directed by the Engineer to see that their specification is the same\n      as adopted in the mix design. They must be stored on clean platform made for the\n       purpose.\n       Concrete shall be weighed batched. The dials of weigh batching unit to be used shall be\n      checked with standard weights periodically. The conversions of weights to volume will be\n       allowed by the Engineer under special circumstances. Despite the design for several\n       mixes the following quantities of cement are the minimum to be used for the different\n       grades of the concrete:-\n      Cement concrete (1:4:8)                 - 3.40 Bags per cum.\n      Cement concrete (1:3:6)                 - 4.40 Bags per cum.\n      Cement concrete (1:2:4)                 - 6.40 Bags per cum.\n      Cement concrete (1:1.5:3)              - 8.00 Bags per cum.\n     RCC             (1:1.5:3)               - 8.00 Bags per cum.\n     RCC              (1:1:2)            -10.80 Bags per cum\n\n14.5.  DEFECTS IN CONCRETE:\n       Immediately on removal of form work, the surface of the concrete shall be examined by\n       the contractor and any bone combs or other imperfections shall be brought to the notice\n        of the Engineer. The acceptability or otherwise of such defective concrete shall be at the\n       sole discretion of the Engineer who may direct the contractor to repair the defective work\n        or ask for demolition and replacement of such defective work at the risk and cost of the\n        contractor."
>     },

Reaffirmed 1991.\n\n            4   1200   (Part  Method of measurement of building and civil engineering work Part 2\n                            II)            (concrete works). (3rd revision) (Amendments 2) Reaffirmed 1991.\n               1974\n            5   1322-1993   Bitumen  felt  for water proofing and damp proofing  (4th revision)\n                                  (previously 13220-1982)\n\n            6   1786-1966   High  strength  deformed  steel  bars  and  wires  for  concrete\n                                 reinforcement.\n            7   1791-1985   Batch type concrete mixers. (2nd revision) Reaffirmed 1990.\n\n            8   2386-1963   Method of test for aggregate for concrete work.\n\n                                     a)  Part 1 particle size and shape (Amendments 2) Reaffirmed\n                                 1990\n                                     b)  Part 2  Estimation  of  deleterious  materials and  organic\n                                           impurities (Amendments 1) Reaffirmed 1990.\n                                        c)  Part 3 Specific gravity, density, voids, absorption and bulking\n                                 – Reaffirmed 1990.l\n                                     d)  Part 4 Mechanical properties (Amendments 3) Reaffirmed\n                                     1990.\n\n            9   2505-1980   General  requirements  for  concrete  vibrators  immersion  type.\n                               Reaffirmed 1993.\n\n           10  2505-1985   General requirements  for screed board  concrete  vibrators.  (1st\n                                    revision) Reaffirmed 1990.\n\n           11  2645-1975    Integral  cement  water   proofing  components   (1st   revision)\n                          (Amendments 1) Reaffirmed 1992.\n\n           12  2686-1977    Cinder as fine aggregate  for use  in lime concrete  (1st revision)\n                          (Amendments 1) Reaffirmed 1992.\n\n           13  3812-1981    Fly ash for use as Pozzolana and admixtures (1st revision) Reaffirmed\n                              1992.\n\n           14  4656-1968   Form vibrators for concrete. Reaffirmed 1991.\n\n           15  8112        43 grade OPC\n\n           16  9103-1979    Admixture for concrete Reaffirmed 1990."

### Jc3abe94f6a36730a

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Clause 8 — Placing and Compacting|Clause 8 — Placing and Compacting]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "8",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 36,
>       "text": "8.    PLACING AND COMPACTING:         Method  of  placing  shall be such as  to\n       preclude segregation and as far as practicable the placing shall be continuous.\n       Special care shall be taken in accordance with IS 456 while laying concrete under\n       extreme weather. Concrete, during the operation of placing shall be thoroughly worked\n       around the reinforcements, embedded fixtures, spaded against corners of the formwork\n      by punning, rodding, or by any other approved means and thoroughly compacted by\n       mechanical vibrators. The number and type of vibrator to be used shall be subject to the\n       approval of the Engineer, and in general immersion type vibrators shall be used."
>     }
>   ],

### Jcbb4e019d209f8c2

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Clause 11 — Construction Joints|Clause 11 — Construction Joints]]

>       "pdf_page": 36,
>       "text": "11.  CONSTRUCTION JOINTS:   Construction joints in exposed concrete work shall be made\n      only where shown on the drawings or directed by the Engineer and shall be in accordance\n      with the details shown or approved by the Engineer. The procedure given in clause\n      20.1.4.2 of IS; 456-1978 shall be followed for general guidance. All laitance shall be\n     removed from the concrete before  it is allowed to fully harden. The removal shall be"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 37,
>       "text": "effected by scrubbing the concrete surface with wire and with bristle brushes and washing\n          down to expose clearly the aggregate. However, care shall be taken to avoid dislodgement\n              of particles of aggregate.\n                       If concrete has been allowed to harden excessively the surface shall be chipped over its\n            whole surface to a depth of at least 10 mm and thereafter thoroughly washed. Before\n              fresh concrete is added on the construction joints, the surface of the old concrete shall\n           be thoroughly wetted and covered with a thin layer of cement mortar 1:2."
>     }

### J28896027b0708774

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works|Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works]]

> {
>   "extraction_method": "geometric_sort",
>   "has_extracted_text": true,
>   "pdf_page": 33,
>   "review_status": "pending_manual_review",
>   "rotation": 0,
>   "text": "180\n       BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\n                                                                              Technical Specifications\n\n\n    PART – B: SPECIFICATIONS FOR REHABILITATION AND RESTORATION WORKS\n\n      Restoration works for safety reasons.\n       1.    The BMRCL may appoint consultant/Engineer to conduct Building Condition Study (BCS)\n          and suggest sequence and methodology of demolition including remedial measures\n             required to be undertaken prior to demolition and/or during demolition and/or post\n             demolition.\n                    It is anticipated that, certain structures will be required to be constructed for safety and\n              functional reasons as a result of Building Condition Study. The structures anticipated are\n               front walls, doors, windows, beams and columns etc.\n          The BMRCL has identified few items which are to be executed in this contract. These\n            items  shall be as per KPWD Common Schedule  of Rates  for the year 2023-24.\n           Measurements will be recorded for only those buildings completed under the supervision\n              of consultant/Engineer appointed for Building Condition Study.\n       2.      All Standards, Technical Specifications and Codes of Practice referred to shall be latest\n             editions including all applicable official amendments and revisions. The contractor shall\n         make available at site all relevant KPWD Specifications and Indian Standard Codes of\n             practice as applicable.\n              In case of discrepancy among Standard codes of practice, Technical Specifications and\n             provisions in Sub clauses in this ‘Employer Requirement, Scope of Work & Technical\n              Specifications’, the order of precedence will be as below:\n\n                               i)  Provision in this ‘Employer Requirement, Scope of Work & Technical\n                    Specifications’\n                                 ii)  Technical Specifications\n                                  iii) KPWD Specifications\n                    v) MORTH Specifications\n                           vii) Standard Codes of Practice\n\n       3.    The utilities which are not to be diverted but require supporting, proper supporting be done\n           so that they are not damaged along their branches. All Precautions shall be taken while\n            handling the utilities. The Contractor shall be liable for any damages in this behalf.\n       4.    Before carrying out the work at site, necessary permissions as required from various local\n            agencies / authorities shall be required to be obtained by the contractor. The Employer\n              shall assist only by way of issue of necessary support letters.\n       5.    The work has to be executed carefully without damaging the existing surface/over\n            ground/underground utilities. The contractor shall execute electrical and plumbing works\n            through Licensed Electrical/Plumbing contractors.\n       6.    The Contractor shall strictly adhere to the working drawings or detail drawings and written\n              instructions of the Engineer.\n\n     TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS\n\n       1.    MATERIALS AND WORKMANSHIP:     The whole of the materials used on the work\n               shall be new and of the best quality of their respective kind. Other articles, tools, plants\n               shall be the best of the several kinds available in the market and shall be used in the best,\n           most workman like, and most substantial manner to the complete satisfaction of the\n             Engineer.\n           The workmanship is to be the best available and of a high standard. The Contractor shall\n             take all steps immediately to make up deficiency if any noticed by the Engineer. Use must\n           be made of a special tradesman in all aspects of the work and allowances must be made\n                in the rates for the same.\n\n              Contractor shall maintain uniform quality and consistency in workmanship throughout the\n             execution of the work.\n\n\n       Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 30 of 37",

### Jb5873f13c1466408

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works|Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works]]

> {
>   "extraction_method": "geometric_sort",
>   "has_extracted_text": true,
>   "pdf_page": 34,
>   "review_status": "pending_manual_review",
>   "rotation": 0,
>   "text": "181\n BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\n                                                                       Technical Specifications\n\n\n        Materials shall be of the best approved quality obtainable and they shall comply with the\n       respective Indian Standard Specification.\n        In case of non-availability of materials in metric sizes, the nearest size in FPS units shall\n      be provided with the prior approval of the Engineer for which neither extra will be paid\n       nor shall any rebate be recovered.\n            It shall be obligatory for the Contractor to furnish Certificate, if demanded by the Engineer,\n       from manufacturer or the material supplier that the work has been carried out by using\n         their material and as per their recommendation.\n       Unless otherwise shown on the drawings or mentioned in the schedule of quantities or\n        special specifications the quality of materials, workmanship, dimensions, etc., shall be as\n        specified here in under.\n         All equipment and facilities for carrying out field tests on materials shall be provided\n        without any extra cost.\n\n2.    TESTING OF WORKS AND MATERIALS:              If required by the Engineer/Employer\n       the Contractor shall arrange to test materials, concrete cubes etc. and portions of the\n      work in order to prove their soundness and sufficiency at his own cost. If, after any such\n        test and in the opinion of the Engineer the materials or workmanship is found defective\n       or unsound, the Contractor shall pull down and re-execute the same at his own cost. All\n       materials deemed defective in the opinion of the Engineer shall be removed from the site\n       immediately.\n\n3.    SAMPLES OF MATERIAL: The Contractor shall deposit at the office of the Engineer\n        for their approval samples of the materials proposed to be used in the works, at least 15\n      days before placing the order supply, and the bulk shall be equal in every respect to the\n      approved samples deposited.\n\n4.    STORAGE:   Safe, dry and proper storage shall be provided for all materials, particularly\n        for materials like cement. Adequate storage should be made at least sufficient for 1 month\n       during active construction.\n      The Contractor shall procure necessary guarantees from the manufactures and furnish\n       the same to the Engineer.\n\n5.    SPECIFICATIONS FOR CONCRETE WORKS\n\n      Concrete General\n      Wherever grade of concrete like M15, M20 etc. are mentioned, the concrete mix shall be\n       designed by the contractor and approval of Engineer for the same shall be obtained.\n      Weigh batching shall be adopted for producing the concrete. Obtaining of concrete from\n       concrete batch mix plant will be permitted. Contractor shall obtain approval of Engineer\n         for each such batching plant. Proportion of ordinary cement concrete will be expressed\n      as l:3:6, 1:2:4, 1:1.5:3 etc. in the specification. The first figure will be the quantity of\n      cement by volume, the second figure will be dry coarse sand (fine aggregate) by volume\n      and the third figure will be the quantity of course aggregate by volume. Cement shall be\n      measured by weight. The weight is to be derived on the basis that 1 cubic meter of\n      cement will weigh 1440 kgs., or one full bag of 50 kgs. will be assumed to be 35 ltrs.\n      when the sand is wet or moist suitable correction for bulking is to be given while\n        proportioning. Engineer may allow measuring cement by volume.\n       Unless otherwise specified the rates for all RCC will be exclusive or reinforcements.\n       Reinforcements will be paid for separate1y.\n       Unless, otherwise stated, for all RCC work the size of course aggregate will be 20 mm\n      and down size.\n\n\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 31 of 37",

>         538.4083251953125,

>         74.16320037841797,

>       ],
>       "block_number": 3,
>       "text": " \nMaterials shall be of the best approved quality obtainable and they shall comply with the \nrespective Indian Standard Specification. \n \nIn case of non-availability of materials in metric sizes, the nearest size in FPS units shall \nbe provided with the prior approval of the Engineer for which neither extra will be paid \nnor shall any rebate be recovered. \n \nIt shall be obligatory for the Contractor to furnish Certificate, if demanded by the Engineer, \nfrom manufacturer or the material supplier that the work has been carried out by using \ntheir material and as per their recommendation.  \n \nUnless otherwise shown on the drawings or mentioned in the schedule of quantities or \nspecial specifications the quality of materials, workmanship, dimensions, etc., shall be as \nspecified here in under. \n \nAll equipment and facilities for carrying out field tests on materials shall be provided \nwithout any extra cost. \n \n2.  \nTESTING OF WORKS AND MATERIALS:  \nIf required by the Engineer/Employer \nthe Contractor shall arrange to test materials, concrete cubes etc. and portions of the \nwork in order to prove their soundness and sufficiency at his own cost. If, after any such \ntest and in the opinion of the Engineer the materials or workmanship is found defective \nor unsound, the Contractor shall pull down and re-execute the same at his own cost. All \nmaterials deemed defective in the opinion of the Engineer shall be removed from the site \nimmediately. \n \n3.  \nSAMPLES OF MATERIAL: The Contractor shall deposit at the office of the Engineer \nfor their approval samples of the materials proposed to be used in the works, at least 15 \ndays before placing the order supply, and the bulk shall be equal in every respect to the \napproved samples deposited. \n \n4.  \nSTORAGE: \nSafe, dry and proper storage shall be provided for all materials, particularly \nfor materials like cement. Adequate storage should be made at least sufficient for 1 month \nduring active construction. \n \nThe Contractor shall procure necessary guarantees from the manufactures and furnish \nthe same to the Engineer. \n \n5.  \nSPECIFICATIONS FOR CONCRETE WORKS \n \n \n \n \nConcrete General \n \nWherever grade of concrete like M15, M20 etc. are mentioned, the concrete mix shall be \ndesigned by the contractor and approval of Engineer for the same shall be obtained. \nWeigh batching shall be adopted for producing the concrete. Obtaining of concrete from \nconcrete batch mix plant will be permitted. Contractor shall obtain approval of Engineer \nfor each such batching plant. Proportion of ordinary cement concrete will be expressed \nas l:3:6, 1:2:4, 1:1.5:3 etc. in the specification. The first figure will be the quantity of \ncement by volume, the second figure will be dry coarse sand (fine aggregate) by volume \nand the third figure will be the quantity of course aggregate by volume. Cement shall be \nmeasured by weight. The weight is to be derived on the basis that 1 cubic meter of \ncement will weigh 1440 kgs., or one full bag of 50 kgs. will be assumed to be 35 ltrs. \nwhen the sand is wet or moist suitable correction for bulking is to be given while \nproportioning. Engineer may allow measuring cement by volume.  \n \nUnless otherwise specified the rates for all RCC will be exclusive or reinforcements. \nReinforcements will be paid for separate1y. \n \nUnless, otherwise stated, for all RCC work the size of course aggregate will be 20 mm \nand down size. \n \n \n \n"
>     },

### J076acca5e09cfd7b

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works|Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works]]

>   "text": "184\n       BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\n                                                                              Technical Specifications\n\n\n             effected by scrubbing the concrete surface with wire and with bristle brushes and washing\n          down to expose clearly the aggregate. However, care shall be taken to avoid dislodgement\n              of particles of aggregate.\n                       If concrete has been allowed to harden excessively the surface shall be chipped over its\n            whole surface to a depth of at least 10 mm and thereafter thoroughly washed. Before\n              fresh concrete is added on the construction joints, the surface of the old concrete shall\n           be thoroughly wetted and covered with a thin layer of cement mortar 1:2.\n\n      12.   VERTICAL JOINTS: At the end of any day's work or run of concrete, the concrete shall\n           be finished of against temporary shutter stop which shall be vertical and securely fixed.\n             This stop shall be removed as soon as the weather permits.\n\n      13.   STRENGTH OF ORDINARY CONCRETE:       The contractors have to see that\n             proper materials are used and the proportion and the correct water cement ratio just\n                sufficient for the workability are maintained to see that the following minimum strength of\n             concrete are obtained.\n           To verify this, test cubes from the concrete used should be made and tested. The\n             frequency of testing and the acceptability criteria will be according to IS 456 and IS 516.\n\n     14   CONCRETE FORMWORK AND CENTERING:\n\n      14.1   General:\n           Form work shall be rigidly constructed of 12 mm thick new plastic coated plywood sheets\n                for column, pedestals, beams, walls and fascia and steel or plywood for roof slab,\n             canopies, floors, cover slabs, retaining walls, etc. Shuttering faces in contact with the\n             concrete shall be free from adhering grout, projecting nails, splits or other defects that\n             are likely to mar the concrete surface. The shuttering shall be erected on battens, beams\n           and steel props properly cross-braced so as to make the form work rigid.\n           Form work shall be erected true to line and levels and to the required shapes as called\n                for and shall be able to carry without deformation the full weight of wet concrete and live\n              loads. It shall also withstand the effect of vibration without deflection, bulging, distortion\n              or loosening of its component parts. Details of centering and form work shall be subject\n               to the approval of the Engineer. The completed form work shall also be subject to the\n             approval by the Engineer before placement of reinforcement. Contractor shall be\n              responsible for sufficiency and adequacy of all form work centering and moulds.\n                 All joints shall be sufficiently tight to prevent leakage of grout. Chamfer fillets shall be\n             provided at all corners wherever called for on the drawings or as directed by the Engineer.\n            Openings may .be given with the approval of Engineer at convenient places for washing\n           down all the rubbish. These are to be closed before concreting.\n\n      14.2  Surface Treatment of Shuttering:  The surfaces of shuttering coming into contact with\n             concrete shall be plastic coated of approved make. The shuttering shall be thoroughly\n            cleaned and coated before each use.\n      14.3  Removal of Form Work:     All formwork shall be removed without shock or vibration\n           and shall be eased off carefully in order to allow the structure to take up its load gradually.\n           Forms shall not be disturbed until, concrete had adequately hardened to take up its own\n             weight and superimposed load coming on it and in no circumstance shall forms be struck\n                 until the concrete reaches strength of at least twice the stress to which the concrete may\n             subjected at the time of striking. The side forms shall be so fixed that while removing\n           them the supporting forms & posts are not disturbed.\n      14.4  Surface Finish of Concrete:\n            Only cement mortar rendering of maximum 6 mm thickness may be permitted as finishing\n               to concrete surface. Imperfect surfaces shall not be patched but shall be repaired by\n            removal and replacement of the member as directed by the Engineer.\n\n\n\n\n       Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 34 of 37",

However, care shall be taken to avoid dislodgement \nof particles of aggregate. \n \nIf concrete has been allowed to harden excessively the surface shall be chipped over its \nwhole surface to a depth of at least 10 mm and thereafter thoroughly washed. Before \nfresh concrete is added on the construction joints, the surface of the old concrete shall \nbe thoroughly wetted and covered with a thin layer of cement mortar 1:2. \n \n12.  \n VERTICAL JOINTS: At the end of any day's work or run of concrete, the concrete shall \nbe finished of against temporary shutter stop which shall be vertical and securely fixed. \nThis stop shall be removed as soon as the weather permits. \n \n13.  \nSTRENGTH OF ORDINARY CONCRETE:  \nThe contractors have to see that \nproper materials are used and the proportion and the correct water cement ratio just \nsufficient for the workability are maintained to see that the following minimum strength of \nconcrete are obtained. \n \nTo verify this, test cubes from the concrete used should be made and tested. The \nfrequency of testing and the acceptability criteria will be according to IS 456 and IS 516. \n \n14 \n CONCRETE FORMWORK AND CENTERING: \n \n14.1 \n General: \n \nForm work shall be rigidly constructed of 12 mm thick new plastic coated plywood sheets \nfor column, pedestals, beams, walls and fascia and steel or plywood for roof slab, \ncanopies, floors, cover slabs, retaining walls, etc. Shuttering faces in contact with the \nconcrete shall be free from adhering grout, projecting nails, splits or other defects that \nare likely to mar the concrete surface. The shuttering shall be erected on battens, beams \nand steel props properly cross-braced so as to make the form work rigid. \n \nForm work shall be erected true to line and levels and to the required shapes as called \nfor and shall be able to carry without deformation the full weight of wet concrete and live \nloads. It shall also withstand the effect of vibration without deflection, bulging, distortion \nor loosening of its component parts. Details of centering and form work shall be subject \nto the approval of the Engineer. The completed form work shall also be subject to the \napproval by the Engineer before placement of reinforcement. Contractor shall be \nresponsible for sufficiency and adequacy of all form work centering and moulds. \n \nAll joints shall be sufficiently tight to prevent leakage of grout. Chamfer fillets shall be \nprovided at all corners wherever called for on the drawings or as directed by the Engineer. \n \nOpenings may .be given with the approval of Engineer at convenient places for washing \ndown all the rubbish. These are to be closed before concreting. \n \n14.2    Surface Treatment of Shuttering: The surfaces of shuttering coming into contact with \nconcrete shall be plastic coated of approved make. The shuttering shall be thoroughly \ncleaned and coated before each use. \n14.3    Removal of Form Work: \nAll formwork shall be removed without shock or vibration \nand shall be eased off carefully in order to allow the structure to take up its load gradually. \n \nForms shall not be disturbed until, concrete had adequately hardened to take up its own \nweight and superimposed load coming on it and in no circumstance shall forms be struck \nuntil the concrete reaches strength of at least twice the stress to which the concrete may \nsubjected at the time of striking. The side forms shall be so fixed that while removing \nthem the supporting forms & posts are not disturbed. \n14.4    Surface Finish of Concrete: \nOnly cement mortar rendering of maximum 6 mm thickness may be permitted as finishing \nto concrete surface. ### J0fa404cc10eb35ce

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works|Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works]]

>   "text": "185\n BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\n                                                                       Technical Specifications\n\n\n            If centering and shuttering is to be paid for separately, measurement will be taken\n       according to IS 1200. The unit area for payment will be one square meter.\n         All aggregate are to conform strictly to IS specification IS 383. The aggregates will be\n       tested as frequently as directed by the Engineer to see that their specification is the same\n      as adopted in the mix design. They must be stored on clean platform made for the\n       purpose.\n       Concrete shall be weighed batched. The dials of weigh batching unit to be used shall be\n      checked with standard weights periodically. The conversions of weights to volume will be\n       allowed by the Engineer under special circumstances. Despite the design for several\n       mixes the following quantities of cement are the minimum to be used for the different\n       grades of the concrete:-\n      Cement concrete (1:4:8)                 - 3.40 Bags per cum.\n      Cement concrete (1:3:6)                 - 4.40 Bags per cum.\n      Cement concrete (1:2:4)                 - 6.40 Bags per cum.\n      Cement concrete (1:1.5:3)              - 8.00 Bags per cum.\n     RCC             (1:1.5:3)               - 8.00 Bags per cum.\n     RCC              (1:1:2)            -10.80 Bags per cum\n\n14.5.  DEFECTS IN CONCRETE:\n       Immediately on removal of form work, the surface of the concrete shall be examined by\n       the contractor and any bone combs or other imperfections shall be brought to the notice\n        of the Engineer. The acceptability or otherwise of such defective concrete shall be at the\n       sole discretion of the Engineer who may direct the contractor to repair the defective work\n        or ask for demolition and replacement of such defective work at the risk and cost of the\n        contractor.\n\n\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 35 of 37",

>       "text": "If centering and shuttering is to be paid for separately, measurement will be taken \naccording to IS 1200. The unit area for payment will be one square meter. \n \nAll aggregate are to conform strictly to IS specification IS 383. The aggregates will be \ntested as frequently as directed by the Engineer to see that their specification is the same \nas adopted in the mix design. They must be stored on clean platform made for the \npurpose. \n \nConcrete shall be weighed batched. The dials of weigh batching unit to be used shall be \nchecked with standard weights periodically. The conversions of weights to volume will be \nallowed by the Engineer under special circumstances. Despite the design for several \nmixes the following quantities of cement are the minimum to be used for the different \ngrades of the concrete:- \n \nCement concrete (1:4:8)  \n \n- 3.40 Bags per cum. \n \nCement concrete (1:3:6)  \n \n- 4.40 Bags per cum. \n \nCement concrete (1:2:4)  \n \n- 6.40 Bags per cum. \n \nCement concrete (1:1.5:3)  \n \n- 8.00 Bags per cum.  \n \n \nRCC                    (1:1.5:3)  \n \n- 8.00 Bags per cum. \n \nRCC                    (1:1:2)  \n \n-10.80 Bags per cum \n \n14.5.   DEFECTS IN CONCRETE: \nImmediately on removal of form work, the surface of the concrete shall be examined by \nthe contractor and any bone combs or other imperfections shall be brought to the notice \nof the Engineer. The acceptability or otherwise of such defective concrete shall be at the \nsole discretion of the Engineer who may direct the contractor to repair the defective work \nor ask for demolition and replacement of such defective work at the risk and cost of the \ncontractor. \n \n \n"

### Jb5c66a5cbbd469d2

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works|Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works]]

>   "text": "182\n       BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\n                                                                              Technical Specifications\n\n\n       6.   CEMENT CONCRETE PLAIN AND REINFORCED:\n\n            A) Cement:  Cement shall comply in every respect with the requirements of the latest\n                 publication of IS: 8112 and unless otherwise specified, ordinary Portland cement shall\n             be used. No other make of cement but that approved by the Engineer will be allowed\n             on works and the source of supply not be changed without approval of the Engineer in\n                   writing. Test certificates to show that the cement used fully complies with the relevant\n                IS specifications shall be submitted to the Engineer and not withstanding this, the\n               Engineer may at their discretion order that the cement brought to site and which they\n            may consider damaged or of doubtful quality for any reasons whatsoever shall be re-\n                tested in an approved testing laboratory and fresh certificate of its soundness shall be\n               produced. Cement ordered for retesting shall not be used for any work pending results\n                  of retest.\n             Cement shall be stored and neatly packed in piles not exceeding 10 bags high in\n               weather proof sheds with raised wooden plank flooring to prevent deterioration by\n             dampness or intrusion of foreign matter. It shall be stored in such a way as to allow\n                the removal and use of cement in chronological order of receipt, i.e. the first received\n               being first used. Cement deteriorated and/or clotted shall not be used on work but shall\n             be removed at once from the site.\n\n           B) Fine Aggregates: Sand shall conform to IS: 383. It shall pass through 1.5 sieve 4.75\n         mm (3/16 B.S) test sieve leaving a residue not more than 5%. It shall be from a natural\n               source or crushed stone screedings.  It shall be washed  if directed to reduce the\n               percentage of deleterious substances to acceptable limits. Sand shall not contain any\n                 trace of salt and sand containing any trace of salt shall be rejected.\n             The fine aggregate for concrete shall be graded within limits as specified in IS: 383\n             and the fineness modules shall range between 2.60 to 3.20. The fine aggregate shall\n               stacked carefully on a clean hard dry surface so that  it will not get mixed up with\n                 deleterious foreign materials. If such a surface is not available a platform of planks or\n                corrugated sheets or brick floor or concrete floor shall be prepared. Sand shall be\n             added in the desired proportion as required for the strength specified, with suitable\n                 correction for bulking.\n\n           C) Coarse Aggregates:    Coarse aggregate shall conform to IS: 383. It shall consist\n                  of crushed or broken stone, 95% of which shall, be retained on 4.75 mm is test sieve.\n                          It shall be obtained from crushed granite, trap, basalt or similar approved stones from\n              approved quarry. Coarse aggregate shall be chemically inert when mixed with cement\n           & shall be angular in shape and free from soft friable thin porous laminated or flaky\n                pieces. It shall be free from dust and other foreign matter. Gravel/shingle of desired\n                grading may be permitted as a substitute in part or full in plain cement concrete if the\n               Engineer are otherwise satisfied about the quality of aggregate.\n\n           D) Water:    Water used for mixing concrete shall be fresh, clean, free from oil, salts,\n                 acids, alkali and shall be in accordance with the clause 4.3 of IS 456 - 1978. The\n                 contractor shall produce test results for approval by the Engineer for the mixing of\n               water proposed to be used on the job, before casting any concrete.\n\n            E) Mixing Of Concrete:    MACHINE  MIXED:  Aggregates  shall  be  accurately\n             measured out in boxes and mixed dry along with required cement, water shall then be\n             added in measured quantity and mixing shall be continued until there is uniform\n                  distribution of the materials and the mass is uniform in color and consistency but in no\n              case shall the mixing be done for less than 2 minutes. Only hopper loading mixer shall\n             be used.\n\n          7.  CONSISTENCY:\n\n\n       Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 32 of 37",

### Jddc885d563a1fcbf

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works|Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works]]

>   "rotation": 0,
>   "text": "187\n BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\n                                                                       Technical Specifications\n\n\n15.   REINFORCEMENT:  Steel reinforcement shall be either mild steel of tested quality high\n        yield strength deformed bars of grade Fe-415 conforming to IS; 1786--1966, Fe-500 or\n      as called for on the drawings Fabric reinforcement where called for shall be of hard drawn\n       mild steel wire mesh conforming to IS: 1566-1967. Bars shall be free from deleterious\n        materials, mill scale, loose rust, oil or paint. The contractor shall submit bar bending\n       schedules for approval of the Engineer prior to commencement of fabrication. These shall\n        indicate the accurate dimensions and bending of bars as called for on the structural\n       drawings. Fabrication shall be accurately done to the dimensions, spacing and ensuring\n      minimum cover as called for on structural drawings. All steel shall be rigidly held in place\n       with 18 gauge annealed steel wire. Cement mortar (1;2) blocks of required shape, M.S.\n       chairs and spacer bars shall be used  in order to ensure accurate positioning  of\n       reinforcement.\n         All reinforcing steel within the limit of a day's polish shall be in place and firmly wired at\n        least one day, prior to the date of pour to permit inspection. The contractor shall also\n       ensure that all conduits and inserts are in position before placing concrete.\n      Hooks where indicated shall be either a complete semi-circular turn with a radius of not\n        less than four and not more than six bar's diameters, plus an extension of at least four\n       bar diameters at the free end, or a 90-degree bend having a radius of not less than 4 bar\n       diameters plus an extension of 12 bar diameters. Unless otherwise shown or specified\n      on the drawings, all lapped by 50 times the diameter of bar. Not more than 33%. (Thirty-\n      Three Percent) of the bars shall have lapped joints at the same location. Only where\n        specifically shown on the drawings, reinforcement shall have welded joints. Rate quoted\n         for steel reinforcement work shall be deemed to include the cost of such welding\n       wherever specified.\n\n16.   STACKING AND STORAGE:       Steel for reinforcement shall be stored in such a\n     way as to prevent distorting and corrosion. Bars of different classifications, sizes and\n      lengths shall be stored separately to facilitate issue in such sizes and lengths to cause to\n     minimum wastage in cutting from standard length.\n\n17.   EARTHWORK INCLUDING GENERAL FILLING, DEWATERING AND BACKFILLING:\n      These Specifications cover the general requirements for earthwork and related works in\n        different types of soils and rocks. The scope of work includes, but is not limited to,\n        clearing of the site; general filling of site; compaction of fill to required degree; excavation\n      and backfilling works; works related to stabilizing of sides of excavations; and necessary\n       dewatering all within the intent of these specifications. The scope shall include provision\n        of access to different parts of the excavated areas, adequate protective safety measures,\n      and proper lighting and necessary signage arrangements.\n       General filling using approved material shall be done in areas as indicated in drawings\n        or as directed by the Engineer.\n      The tenderer should acquaint him with the site for assessing the efforts necessary for\n       completing the work as per these specifications.\n\n\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 37 of 37",

>       "text": "15.  \nREINFORCEMENT: Steel reinforcement shall be either mild steel of tested quality high \nyield strength deformed bars of grade Fe-415 conforming to IS; 1786--1966, Fe-500 or \nas called for on the drawings Fabric reinforcement where called for shall be of hard drawn \nmild steel wire mesh conforming to IS: 1566-1967. Bars shall be free from deleterious \nmaterials, mill scale, loose rust, oil or paint. The contractor shall submit bar bending \nschedules for approval of the Engineer prior to commencement of fabrication. These shall \nindicate the accurate dimensions and bending of bars as called for on the structural \ndrawings. Fabrication shall be accurately done to the dimensions, spacing and ensuring \nminimum cover as called for on structural drawings. All steel shall be rigidly held in place \nwith 18 gauge annealed steel wire. Cement mortar (1;2) blocks of required shape, M.S. \nchairs and spacer bars shall be used in order to ensure accurate positioning of \nreinforcement. \n \nAll reinforcing steel within the limit of a day's polish shall be in place and firmly wired at \nleast one day, prior to the date of pour to permit inspection. The contractor shall also \nensure that all conduits and inserts are in position before placing concrete. \n \nHooks where indicated shall be either a complete semi-circular turn with a radius of not \nless than four and not more than six bar's diameters, plus an extension of at least four \nbar diameters at the free end, or a 90-degree bend having a radius of not less than 4 bar \ndiameters plus an extension of 12 bar diameters. Unless otherwise shown or specified \non the drawings, all lapped by 50 times the diameter of bar. Not more than 33%. (Thirty-\nThree Percent) of the bars shall have lapped joints at the same location. Only where \nspecifically shown on the drawings, reinforcement shall have welded joints. Rate quoted \nfor steel reinforcement work shall be deemed to include the cost of such welding \nwherever specified. \n \n16.  \n STACKING AND STORAGE: \nSteel for reinforcement shall be stored in such a \nway as to prevent distorting and corrosion. Bars of different classifications, sizes and \nlengths shall be stored separately to facilitate issue in such sizes and lengths to cause to \nminimum wastage in cutting from standard length. \n \n17.  \nEARTHWORK INCLUDING GENERAL FILLING, DEWATERING AND BACKFILLING: \nThese Specifications cover the general requirements for earthwork and related works in \ndifferent types of soils and rocks. The scope of work includes, but is not limited to, \nclearing of the site; general filling of site; compaction of fill to required degree; excavation \nand backfilling works; works related to stabilizing of sides of excavations; and necessary \ndewatering all within the intent of these specifications. The scope shall include provision \nof access to different parts of the excavated areas, adequate protective safety measures, \nand proper lighting and necessary signage arrangements.     \nGeneral filling using approved material shall be done in areas as indicated in drawings \nor as directed by the Engineer.  \nThe tenderer should acquaint him with the site for assessing the efforts necessary for \ncompleting the work as per these specifications. \n"

### J47015c69706b0a82

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works|Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4B: Specifications for Rehabilitation and Restoration Works]]

>   "text": "183\n BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\n                                                                       Technical Specifications\n\n\n      Only sufficient water giving due allowance for the moisture content of aggregate shall be\n      added to the cement and aggregates during mixing to produce a mixture of sufficient\n        workability to enable  it to be well consolidated, to be worked into the corners of the\n       shuttering and around the reinforcements where there is reinforcement to give the\n       specified finish and to have specified strength.\n       Normally for every 50 kg of cement total water including moisture content of aggregate\n       should not be more than 34 liters for 1:3:6 mix 32 liters for 1:2:4 mix. 30 liters for 1:1½:3\n      and 27 liters for 1:1:2.\n            If  difficulty be experienced in placing the concrete of specified mix and approved\n       consistency between and below reinforcement bars, in the bottom of beams and similar\n        situations, the concrete shall have improved workability by increasing the proportion of\n       water and corresponding additional quantity of cement and using aggregates of smaller\n        size than specified as directed by Engineer for which no extra be paid.\n      The consistency shall be determined by making trial mixtures with dried aggregates, or\n      when so instructed by test of laboratory made test cubes under the direction of Engineer.\n       Consistency may be measured by slump test using a standard cone or the Engineer may\n        direct the use of any other means of testing the consistency.\n            If the apparatus used for the slump test is a standard cone when filled. It shall be raised\n         vertically clear of the concrete and the measurement of the slump shall be 300 mm minus\n       the height of the slumped cone of concrete. Care shall be taken to prevent vibration of\n       the samples being tested. The following slumps shall be adopted for different kind of\n       works:\n\n     WITH/WITHOUT VIBRATOR\n        1.   Mass concrete in RCC foundation, footings & retaining walls. 10 mm to 25 mm 80\n       mm\n        2.   Beams, slabs and columns 25 mm to 40 mm 100 mm to 125 mm simply reinforced\n        3.   Thin RCC section or 40 mm to 50 mm 125 mm to 150 mm section with congested\n              steel\n\n8.    PLACING AND COMPACTING:         Method  of  placing  shall be such as  to\n       preclude segregation and as far as practicable the placing shall be continuous.\n       Special care shall be taken in accordance with IS 456 while laying concrete under\n       extreme weather. Concrete, during the operation of placing shall be thoroughly worked\n       around the reinforcements, embedded fixtures, spaded against corners of the formwork\n      by punning, rodding, or by any other approved means and thoroughly compacted by\n       mechanical vibrators. The number and type of vibrator to be used shall be subject to the\n       approval of the Engineer, and in general immersion type vibrators shall be used.\n\n9.   TRANSPORTING:    Concrete shall be conveyed from the place of mixing to the place\n       of final deposit as rapidly as practicable by methods which will prevent segregation or loss\n       of any of the ingredients. If segregation does occur during transport, the concrete shall be\n      remixed before being placed. Normally not more than 30 minutes shall lapse between\n      mixing and consolidation in position.\n\n10.   CURING:      All cement concrete after laying shall be protected from damages,  till  it\n      sets and shall be cured thereafter for not less than 10 days. The work shall be protected\n      from drying wind and direct sun rays. Water used for curing shall be free from sediments\n       of any kind and generally fit for drinking.\n\n11.  CONSTRUCTION JOINTS:   Construction joints in exposed concrete work shall be made\n      only where shown on the drawings or directed by the Engineer and shall be in accordance\n      with the details shown or approved by the Engineer. The procedure given in clause\n      20.1.4.2 of IS; 456-1978 shall be followed for general guidance. All laitance shall be\n     removed from the concrete before  it is allowed to fully harden. The removal shall be\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 33 of 37",

>       "block_number": 3,
>       "text": "Only sufficient water giving due allowance for the moisture content of aggregate shall be \nadded to the cement and aggregates during mixing to produce a mixture of sufficient \nworkability to enable it to be well consolidated, to be worked into the corners of the \nshuttering and around the reinforcements where there is reinforcement to give the \nspecified finish and to have specified strength. \n \nNormally for every 50 kg of cement total water including moisture content of aggregate \nshould not be more than 34 liters for 1:3:6 mix 32 liters for 1:2:4 mix. 30 liters for 1:1½:3 \nand 27 liters for 1:1:2. \n \nIf difficulty be experienced in placing the concrete of specified mix and approved \nconsistency between and below reinforcement bars, in the bottom of beams and similar \nsituations, the concrete shall have improved workability by increasing the proportion of \nwater and corresponding additional quantity of cement and using aggregates of smaller \nsize than specified as directed by Engineer for which no extra be paid. \n \nThe consistency shall be determined by making trial mixtures with dried aggregates, or \nwhen so instructed by test of laboratory made test cubes under the direction of Engineer. \nConsistency may be measured by slump test using a standard cone or the Engineer may \ndirect the use of any other means of testing the consistency. \n \nIf the apparatus used for the slump test is a standard cone when filled. It shall be raised \nvertically clear of the concrete and the measurement of the slump shall be 300 mm minus \nthe height of the slumped cone of concrete. Care shall be taken to prevent vibration of \nthe samples being tested. The following slumps shall be adopted for different kind of \nworks: \n \n"
>     },
>     {
>       "bbox": [
>         108.00117492675781,
>         364.922607421875,
>         254.10882568359375,
>         375.2781066894531
>       ],
>       "block_number": 4,
>       "text": "WITH/WITHOUT VIBRATOR \n"
>     },
>     {
>       "bbox": [
>         72.00194549560547,
>         377.7626037597656,
>         542.2289428710938,
>         628.4780883789062
>       ],
>       "block_number": 5,
>       "text": "1. \nMass concrete in RCC foundation, footings & retaining walls. 10 mm to 25 mm 80 \nmm  \n2. \nBeams, slabs and columns 25 mm to 40 mm 100 mm to 125 mm simply reinforced  \n3. \nThin RCC section or 40 mm to 50 mm 125 mm to 150 mm section with congested \nsteel  \n \n8. \nPLACING AND COMPACTING: \n \nMethod of placing shall be such as to \npreclude segregation and as far as practicable the placing shall be continuous. \n \nSpecial care shall be taken in accordance with IS 456 while laying concrete under \nextreme weather. Concrete, during the operation of placing shall be thoroughly worked \naround the reinforcements, embedded fixtures, spaded against corners of the formwork \nby punning, rodding, or by any other approved means and thoroughly compacted by \nmechanical vibrators. The number and type of vibrator to be used shall be subject to the \napproval of the Engineer, and in general immersion type vibrators shall be used. \n \n9.       TRANSPORTING: \nConcrete shall be conveyed from the place of mixing to the place \nof final deposit as rapidly as practicable by methods which will prevent segregation or loss \nof any of the ingredients. If segregation does occur during transport, the concrete shall be \nremixed before being placed. Normally not more than 30 minutes shall lapse between \nmixing and consolidation in position. \n"
>     },
>     {
>       "bbox": [
>         72.00212097167969,
>         630.7225952148438,
>         539.2296752929688,
>         767.5580444335938
>       ],
>       "block_number": 6,
>       "text": " \n10.  \n CURING: \nAll cement concrete after laying shall be protected from damages, till it \nsets and shall be cured thereafter for not less than 10 days. The work shall be protected \nfrom drying wind and direct sun rays. Water used for curing shall be free from sediments \nof any kind and generally fit for drinking. \n \n11. \nCONSTRUCTION JOINTS: \nConstruction joints in exposed concrete work shall be made \nonly where shown on the drawings or directed by the Engineer and shall be in accordance \nwith the details shown or approved by the Engineer. The procedure given in clause \n20.1.4.2 of IS; 456-1978 shall be followed for general guidance. All laitance shall be \nremoved from the concrete before it is allowed to fully harden. The removal shall be \n"

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Clause 2 — Testing of Works and Materials|Clause 2 — Testing of Works and Materials]]

> {

>       "text": "2.    TESTING OF WORKS AND MATERIALS:              If required by the Engineer/Employer\n       the Contractor shall arrange to test materials, concrete cubes etc. and portions of the\n      work in order to prove their soundness and sufficiency at his own cost. If, after any such\n        test and in the opinion of the Engineer the materials or workmanship is found defective\n       or unsound, the Contractor shall pull down and re-execute the same at his own cost. All\n       materials deemed defective in the opinion of the Engineer shall be removed from the site\n       immediately."
>     }

### J6df236b4e4c87756

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4B#Clause 17 — Earthwork Including General Filling, Dewatering and Backfilling|Clause 17 — Earthwork Including General Filling, Dewatering and Backfilling]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "17",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 40,
>       "text": "17.   EARTHWORK INCLUDING GENERAL FILLING, DEWATERING AND BACKFILLING:\n      These Specifications cover the general requirements for earthwork and related works in\n        different types of soils and rocks. The scope of work includes, but is not limited to,\n        clearing of the site; general filling of site; compaction of fill to required degree; excavation\n      and backfilling works; works related to stabilizing of sides of excavations; and necessary\n       dewatering all within the intent of these specifications. The scope shall include provision\n        of access to different parts of the excavated areas, adequate protective safety measures,\n      and proper lighting and necessary signage arrangements.\n       General filling using approved material shall be done in areas as indicated in drawings\n        or as directed by the Engineer.\n      The tenderer should acquaint him with the site for assessing the efforts necessary for\n       completing the work as per these specifications."
>     }
